The #82 marker cases gain BLR parser fixtures (security review L3): a BLR string literal holding a marker with `print_blr = true`, and a marker in a BLR attachment's process name; each asserts that no payload, log line or metric carries the marker.
6. **Routines run by a BLR request** (security review L1). A routine or trigger record of the same binding and transaction written after an `EXECUTE_BLR` cannot fold into it (it was written before them). Such records pend as an R25 group and fold into the **next** statement finish of that binding and transaction, which can be an unrelated statement, a case (b) statement included. The "no event" rules (R8 case (b), and "no object left after the filters") apply to that statement only **after** the union with the folded group, so a folded group's objects are never dropped by them. With no following statement finish, R25 item 3 applies (the block-ful part one event, the block-less part ambiguous). docs/08 states that objects of routines run by a BLR request can appear on an unrelated statement's event, and that a selectable procedure's largest row count can raise `volume.large_result` there (over-reporting only). Measured in 3n.

**Forging.** A forged `EXECUTE_BLR` header that captures a real statement trailer does not fit (`records fetched`): ambiguous. One that captures a real routine trailer (a performance line and a block) fits, and the block's relations are reported with `*`, always reported, under the forged binding's principal: the accepted misattribution residual, and the routine's calling statement still reports the nested reads (obligation 7). A forged `COMPILE_BLR` can capture only a block-less routine trailer, which touched no record: nothing is hidden.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Report only the block's relations, without `*`: a request that reads another relation after its start would lose it. (b) Use the block's `Natural` count as `rows`: it is 1 for a 10 000-row scan. (c) Keep `records fetched` expected: every BLR read ambiguous, capping the level for 24 h at each use.

**Residual risks** (for docs/08): a BLR read is reported with the relations its request touched at its start, `*` and the always-reported mark, never with a row count or a volume signal.

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol.

### R34. `ALTER SESSION RESET` with connect and disconnect triggers (measurement 3k)

**Measured** (both versions, through the API with no active transaction; `isql` cannot run it): on a database with `ON CONNECT` and `ON DISCONNECT` triggers that each read `T1`, `ALTER SESSION RESET` fires the `ON DISCONNECT` trigger, then the `ON CONNECT` trigger (two `EXECUTE_TRIGGER_FINISH` records, each with the block `T1 Natural 1`), then writes its own statement record in R8 case (b)'s form (no `Statement <n>:` line, `0 records fetched`, a performance line) **with a block**, the sum of the triggers' (`T1 Natural 2`).

R24 item 3 keeps `ALTER SESSION RESET` among the kinds with "no block", so this record is ambiguous. R15 item 2 also fails for both trigger records: `ON CONNECT` there is not in the binding's first transaction, and `ON DISCONNECT` is not followed by the binding's `DETACH_DATABASE`.

**Proposed**, amending R24 item 3, R8 case (b), R8 item 3 (case (b): no event) and R15 item 2:

1. **`ALTER SESSION RESET` may carry a block.** It joins `SET TRANSACTION`, `COMMIT` and `ROLLBACK` in R24 item 3: with exactly `0 records fetched` and no parameter line, a block is well formed (no split window) and gives an event as R24 item 2 says (the block's relations under the filters, system relations outside the always-reported lists as noise, always-reported relations with their mark, no `rows`, under the binding's principal; never the agent's own when a relation is left). Without a block it produces no event, as case (b) says. The duplicate of the triggers' events is kept, not folded (R24 item 4). Every other kind of R8 case (b) (`SAVEPOINT`, `SET BIND` and the other session-management statements) keeps "no block".
2. **Reset-fired database triggers.** An `ON DISCONNECT` record followed by an `ON CONNECT` record of the same binding, both followed, within R25 item 6's hold bounds, by that binding's well-formed `ALTER SESSION RESET` statement record, meets R15 item 2's kind condition (in place of "the binding's first transaction" and "followed by its `DETACH_DATABASE`"). Either trigger may be absent (a database with only one of them). The trailer and token conditions of R15 item 2 stay. Its transaction condition stays too: 3k did not record the transaction lines of the reset-fired trigger records, so the lot 3 matrix test records them (obligation 3n); if they differ from R15's (a transaction started and ended around each trigger), the rule is revised in an implementation note before the Audit lot merges, and until then a reset-fired trigger record that fails it is ambiguous (loud). The records then become events as R15 item 4 says. A reset-shaped pair that no `ALTER SESSION RESET` record follows within the bounds keeps R15 item 5 (ambiguous; a block-less one opens a window).

**Forging.** A forged `ALTER SESSION RESET` header (case (b) form) can capture a real statement trailer with `0 records fetched` and a block (a scan that matched nothing): its relations are reported under the forged binding's principal, with no row count lost (the captured trailer says 0), the misattribution residual of R24's forging analysis. A forged trigger pair needs the binding's token (R15).

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Keep "no block": every reset on a database with connect or disconnect triggers opens a split window, and the agent's own sessions are among those that may reset. (b) Fold the reset's block into the trigger events: rejected for the reason of R24 item 4.

**Residual risks** (for docs/08, security review N2): a reset on a database with owner-defined connect or disconnect triggers repeats the triggers' reads, under the session's principal and with no row count, on the reset record's event as well as on the triggers' (R15's residuals apply to those triggers).

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol.

### R35. Confirmations (measurements 3i and 3k)

- **R22 confirmed** (3i, both versions): every full read has N = C; every byte-budget and row-limit read fits Q ≤ N ≤ Q + M with M = 64 (measured N = Q + 64).
- **The request synchronization error's cause** (R22 item 6): it is written only when the row-limit cut's last request (the limit plus one) **reaches the end of the result**; cuts that do not reach it write no error. Its shape is exact: `ERROR AT JResultSet::fetchNext`, the sole code `335544364`, no trailer. R22 item 6's follow-up stays: whether the connector lot removes the cause (not asking for one row beyond the limit when the end can be reached) is decided there, in an implementation note that also withdraws the follow-up.
- **A client cancel during a pending fetch is answered** (R22 item 3): `op_cancel` was answered by `isc_cancelled` in 249 to 321 ms, the session stayed usable, and the server wrote, at the cancel, a `FAILED EXECUTE_STATEMENT_FINISH` (the text, `0 records fetched`, a performance line, a partial block of the read table), then `ERROR AT JStatement::openCursor` (or `JResultSet::fetchNext` for a cancel during a fetch) with the **sole** code `335544794`. It is distinguishable from the server's statement timeout, which writes both `335544794` and `335545129` at `JResultSet::fetchNext`. **Amending R22 items 4 and 5** (security review L5):
  - a credit ends **cancelled** only when the connector sent `op_cancel` and the pending request was answered by `isc_cancelled`;
  - a `FAILED EXECUTE_STATEMENT_FINISH` matching a **cancelled** credit is the agent's own under R22 item 5's conditions, with the **failed** bound (0 ≤ N ≤ Q + M) and the text's may-read set plus its metadata-loading set;
  - the credit then holds one error follow-up: an `ERROR` record at `JStatement::openCursor` or `JResultSet::fetchNext` whose sole code is `335544794`, with R22 item 7's exact shape, and only as **the next record of that binding after the credited `FAILED` finish**;
  - a cancel whose pending request was answered by rows or by the end of the result stays on R22 item 3's stale-cancel path: a `FAILED` finish there is not the agent's own;
  - a cancel the connector did not send (for example `DELETE FROM MON$STATEMENTS` by a holder of `MONITOR_ANY_ATTACHMENT`) ends the credit as **error**: its records are not the agent's own (fail closed, R22 item 4).
- **The stale cancel and the drop delay** (R22 item 3, L7 and N5): no cancel outliving an answered fetch was seen, but the race was not forced. R22 item 3's clean end of the session after a stale cancel whose `DSQL_drop` succeeds stands until the connector lot forces it. The drop delay was not measured again: the lifetime margin of R22 item 3 is unchanged.
- **The connector's attachment line** has the character-set field `UTF8` and the client line `databastion-agent:0`; it is matched by the own-attachment rule (decision 6, R1), not by R29's `<internal>` shape, which requires `NONE`.
- **User DML on system tables is refused** (3k, R24 item 2's premise): `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `INSERT` on `RDB$RELATIONS` and `RDB$DATABASE`, with a `FAILED EXECUTE_STATEMENT_FINISH` carrying the metadata-loading block, then `ERROR AT JStatement::execute`; both are handled by the existing rules.
- **An API commit's `COMMIT_TRANSACTION` does not carry a commit trigger's block** (3k): the trigger's read is its own R15 record, written before the end record. R24 item 2's case of a user relation in a transaction-end block does not occur on 4.0.7 and 5.0.4; the rule stays for a version that writes one. The `ON TRANSACTION_COMMIT` name lines (5.0.4 `Trigger TR_TXC (ON TRANSACTION_COMMIT):`, 4.0.7 the tab form) match R14 item 2's grammar.

### R36. Lot 3 obligations from the contract reviews

1. **A record that raises a signal always gives an event** (review of #380, the `signature.backup_database` registry text). A record whose rules raise a signal produces an access event under its principal even when the filters leave it no object, for example `ALTER DATABASE BEGIN BACKUP`, whose block holds only system relations. When no object is left, the event names `*` of its database and is always reported; an event that carries `signature.backup_database` always names `*` and is always reported, as R27 item 1 says for services backups, and now also for the four DDL forms (the registry text of #380 says "reported as a read of *, always reported" only for the services backup and the `gbak` hint; "Protocol" below). The "no object left, no event" rules (R8 item 3, R15 item 4, R24 item 2) apply only to records that raise no signal. The Audit lot's parser tests cover `ALTER DATABASE BEGIN BACKUP`, `CREATE SHADOW`, `ALTER DATABASE ADD FILE` and `ALTER DATABASE ADD DIFFERENCE FILE`, each block-ful (system relations only) and block-less: exactly one event with `signature.backup_database`, `*`, the always-reported mark and the record's principal.
2. **Trace-setting labels ranked** (review of #377). `audit.trace_settings_missing` can need more labels than the 16 a note carries (proof 4, R21 and R27 item 2 list 18 settings). The agent core's truncation rank (`SEVERITY` in `agent/crates/core/src/notes.rs`) lists the Firebird trace-setting labels so that `connection_id`, `enabled`, `log_filename`, `include_filter` and `exclude_filter` are never cut: these say that the applying section does not apply or that records are filtered out, while the others name a missing record kind. Proposed order: `connection_id`, `enabled`, `log_filename`, `include_filter`, `exclude_filter`, `log_blr_requests`, `print_perf`, `time_threshold`, `log_statement_finish`, `log_connections`, `log_transactions`, `log_initfini`, `log_procedure_finish`, `log_function_finish`, `log_trigger_finish`, `log_errors`, `log_sweep`, `log_services`. A test sends all 18 missing and asserts that the first five and `log_blr_requests` (which makes BLR reads visible, R32 item 1; security review N5) are kept. The level is Limited whatever is cut, so the rank only decides what the operator sees first; agent side only.

### Obligations still open after this refinement

Each is measured before the pull request that relies on it:

- **3n** (R32, R33, R34), on 4.0.7 and 5.0.4, before the Audit lot merges, from a throwaway harness for the BLR and utility cases (never the connector):
  - every utility attachment tag (`gbak`, `gfix`, `gstat`) with an ordinary account and with the matching system privilege, for BLR and for DSQL reads: what the server allows, and whether any record names the read (M2);
  - whether a utility attachment can be told apart (its `ATTACH_DATABASE` line, `MON$ATTACHMENTS`), and whether DSQL statement records are written under it (M1); whether the minimal account can read `RDB$ROLES.RDB$SYSTEM_PRIVILEGES` and the `RDB$ADMIN` grants (R32 item 5);
  - the ordinary account's refusal of the utility attachment, kept as an engine-matrix regression test (L2);
  - the `COMPILE_BLR` and `EXECUTE_BLR` shapes in SuperServer and SuperClassic, with their `FAILED` and `UNAUTHORIZED` forms (L2); routine and trigger records run by a BLR request (R33 item 6);
  - the transaction lines of reset-fired database-trigger records (R34 item 2);
  - here or in the connector lot: a server-side socket in `/proc/net/tcp` for a `service_mgr` backup's loopback attachment (N7).
- **3i remainder** (R22 item 3), in the connector lot: the stale-cancel race, forced; the own-statement table rows of the cancelled and synchronization-error paths.
- **3l** and **3m** with **8g** (unchanged, refinement of 2026-10-26 c), and obligation 15's open items.

### Protocol (P10-C)

**No protocol change in this refinement.** R32 withdraws the conditional label of R27's "Protocol" paragraph: no `TargetNoteLabel`, note code, field, signal, capability token or metric is added. **One follow-up registry text change** (security review L4): the `signature.backup_database` text merged in #380 says "each reported as a read of *, always reported" only for the services backup and the `gbak` hint, while R36 item 1 makes every `signature.backup_database` event name `*` and be always reported (the four DDL forms too). Once this refinement is accepted, a P10-C pull request widens that text, with the `security-reviewer` review that every `shared/protocol/` change requires (AGENTS.md); no label. The BLR shapes, the reset rules, the cancelled-credit follow-up, the signal rule and the label rank are parser, connector and core rules of the agent; BLR and reset events leave out the optional `rows`, as database-trigger and transaction-record events do. The Audit lot keeps its `security-reviewer` review (AGENTS.md).

### Security review of 2026-10-27 (refinement of 2026-10-27)

Review of the first version of this refinement (`94d4133`); verdict: **no Critical or High finding, ready once M1 and M2 are applied**. Every finding is applied in this revision:

| Finding | Fix |
|---|---|
| **M1**: the utility residual is silent, not "reported as a backup": any client can set the utility tag over TCP with any process name and no services call | R32 item 2: stated plainly (no event, no signal, no `*`), the "next to the backup detection" framing dropped; docs/08 and the user guide: utility privilege holders are untraced readers within their object rights; R32 item 5: a narrowed detection for principals known to hold a utility privilege, decided after 3n; 3n: whether a utility attachment can be told apart and whether DSQL records are written under it; open question 1 |
| **M2**: 3j measured only the `gbak` tag | R32 item 2: scope stated as the owner, `RDB$ADMIN` and holders of any utility system privilege until 3n; 3n: every utility tag with an ordinary account and the matching privilege, BLR and DSQL, both versions |
| **L1** | R33 item 6: the "no event" rules apply only after the union with a folded group; docs/08: BLR-run routine objects on an unrelated statement, `volume.large_result` there over-reporting only |
| **L2** | R33 items 1 and 3: `FAILED` and `UNAUTHORIZED` BLR forms ambiguous until 3n; 3n covers SuperClassic, the ordinary-account refusal (also a matrix regression test) and the reset-fired triggers' transaction lines |
| **L3** | R33 item 5: #82 BLR marker fixtures (a string literal with `print_blr = true`, a process name), asserting no payload, log line or metric carries the marker |
| **L4** | "Protocol": one follow-up registry text change widening the #380 text to every `signature.backup_database` event, with a `security-reviewer` review once accepted; no label |
| **L5** | R35: "cancelled" defined (an `op_cancel` answered by `isc_cancelled`); the follow-up only as the next record of the binding after the credited `FAILED` finish; a cancel answered by rows stays on the stale-cancel path; an external cancel ends **error**, not own |
| **N2** | R34 residual: resets repeat owner-defined triggers' reads with no row count |
| **N5** | R36 item 2: `log_blr_requests` ranked sixth and asserted by the label test |
| **N7** | R32 item 4 and 3n: a server-side socket for a `service_mgr` backup's loopback attachment, else rule 9 makes services backups ambiguous |

### Open questions (for the maintainer, refinement of 2026-10-27)

Each with the answer this refinement proposed, revised to the security review of 2026-10-27. **Decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-27: every proposed answer accepted**, question 1 included.

1. **R32 items 1, 2 and 5**: record that R27 item 4 (ii) does not apply (Partial not withheld, no label); restate the accepted utility residual as **silent** (BLR reads under a utility attachment, set by any client over TCP, give no event, signal or `*`), scoped to the owner, `RDB$ADMIN` and holders of any utility system privilege until 3n, within their object rights; and keep the narrowed detection (an always-reported `*` event for a transaction with no statement record on an attachment of such a principal, not explained by a `START_SERVICE`) as an option decided after 3n?
   **For the maintainer.** Proposed: keep Partial with the residual stated plainly in docs/08 and the user guide, since it is open only to accounts that already administer or back up the database; decide the narrowed detection after 3n. Rejecting would withhold Partial wherever such accounts exist.
2. **R32 item 3**: drop R27 item 5 (and R29 item 3), or keep it as written?
   **Proposed**: drop. The end-record counters do not include the reads it was meant to catch, so it detects nothing measured; it was never a condition for any level.
3. **R33**: `COMPILE_BLR` block-less with no event; `EXECUTE_BLR` with a performance line and a block or none, reported as the block's relations plus `*`, always reported, with no row count; `FAILED` and `UNAUTHORIZED` forms ambiguous until 3n, then an always-reported `*` event; the "no event" rules applied after the union with a folded group?
   **Proposed**: yes. The block is written at the request start, so it is incomplete and its counters are not a row count.
4. **R34**: `ALTER SESSION RESET` may carry a block, reported as R24 item 2; reset-fired `ON DISCONNECT` / `ON CONNECT` records meet R15's kind condition when the reset record follows, with their transaction lines measured in 3n?
   **Proposed**: yes. Otherwise every reset on such a database opens a split window.
5. **R35**: own `FAILED` finishes and a sole-`335544794` follow-up, as the next record of the binding, only for credits whose `op_cancel` was answered by `isc_cancelled`; a cancel answered by rows on the stale-cancel path; an external cancel not own?
   **Proposed**: yes. 3i records the form, and it is distinguishable from the server's timeout.
6. **R36 and the protocol**: a signal always gives an event (`*`, always reported, when no object is left; always for `signature.backup_database`), the trace-setting label rank of item 2, and the follow-up widening of the `signature.backup_database` registry text with a `security-reviewer` review?
   **Proposed**: yes.

## Refinement (2026-10-30, accepted by the maintainer on 2026-10-30): the lot 3 implementation

Status: **Accepted by the maintainer on 2026-10-30 with the proposed answers** to every open question (1 to 6). **Proposed on 2026-10-30 and revised after its [security review of 2026-10-30](#security-review-of-2026-10-30-refinement-of-2026-10-30)** (sound after changes: no Critical or High finding; M1 to M3, L1 to L5, N1, N2 and N5 applied in that revision). It records what the lot 3 implementation (the Audit lot, branch `feat/p10-b-firebird-audit`, not merged) found against the accepted text, measured on 4.0.7 and 5.0.4 in the trace logs recorded for its parser tests (`agent/crates/connector-firebird/testdata/`: a 5.0.4 `isql` and `gbak` session, 4.0.7 and 5.0.4 trigger and sweep sessions) and in the `dev/firebird/it.sh` workload. It also lists the interim simplifications of that lot, each with a proposed answer: an accepted residual until a follow-up lot, or a requirement before the merge. It amends, by reference:

- the refinement of 2026-10-26 b: R20 ("A statement finish without a `Statement <n>:` line");
- decision 6: "Signals" (`shape.full_table_read`) and "What is kept" (the time offset);
- decision 6 "What is kept" also for the conditions under which the time offset is learned (R40 item 2, security review M3);
- for the interim period of R39 only, with no change to their text: R1 rules 2 and 9, R4 (the readers check), R12 item 5, R13 items 1 and 2 with R23, R22 items 5 to 8 with R35, R25 items 7 and 9, and R29.

The accepted text and the five accepted refinements stay as they are; where this refinement differs from them, this refinement applies. **No protocol change.** ROADMAP: [phase 10](../ROADMAP.md#phase-10--engine-scope-wave-2-sqlite-firebird), P10-B.

### R37. DDL and DCL statement records without a `Statement <n>:` line

**Measured** (4.0.7 and 5.0.4, `isql`): `isql` writes the finish record of every DDL and DCL statement it ran in these sessions **without a `Statement <n>:` line** (the form R20 recorded for `SET TRANSACTION`, `COMMIT` and `ROLLBACK`), with `0 records fetched`, a performance line and no parameter line. Most kinds also carry a **block of system relations**: `CREATE TABLE` (`RDB$DATABASE`, `RDB$FIELDS`, `RDB$INDICES`, `RDB$RELATION_FIELDS`, `RDB$RELATIONS`, `RDB$USER_PRIVILEGES`, `RDB$GENERATORS`, `RDB$CHARACTER_SETS`, `RDB$COLLATIONS`, `RDB$PUBLICATIONS`, `RDB$TRIGGERS`), `CREATE GLOBAL TEMPORARY TABLE`, `CREATE PROCEDURE` and `CREATE FUNCTION` (`RDB$PROCEDURES`, `RDB$FUNCTIONS` and their parameter relations), `CREATE TRIGGER` (`RDB$TRIGGERS`, `RDB$INDICES`), and `ALTER DATABASE BEGIN BACKUP` / `END BACKUP`, `ALTER DATABASE ADD DIFFERENCE FILE`, `CREATE SHADOW` and `DROP SHADOW` (`RDB$FILES`). `CREATE USER … PASSWORD` and `DROP USER` are block-less. When a DDL trigger touched a user table (R26: `DDL_AFTER_CT FOR DDL_LOG`), the DDL record's block also names that table.

R20 as accepted makes that form well formed only for R8 case (b), with no block (R24 item 3 and R34 item 1 add blocks for their kinds). So every DDL or DCL record that `isql` writes is **ambiguous**: always reported with `*`, counted in `audit.log_records_ambiguous`, and capping the level for 24 h (proof 8). Each schema change made with `isql` would deny Partial for a day.

What a DDL or DCL record becomes when it is well formed is unchanged (R40 item 1): an event of its kind under its principal, naming `*` of its database with the always-reported mark and the block's relations under the filters. The ambiguous event differs from it only by the fix-3 attribution, the count and the level cap. Removing those is all this part gains.

**Proposed**, amending R20:

1. **A well-formed DDL / DCL shape for the form without a statement line.** An `EXECUTE_STATEMENT_FINISH` with no `Statement <n>:` line is well formed when all of these hold:
   - the record is otherwise not ambiguous (header, token, binding, attachment line, no header-shaped line in the text, text not cut, no unterminated construct at its end, R12 item 3);
   - its text parses (R8 item 1's meaning), and the statement analysis gives the kind DDL or DCL in both dialect readings;
   - exactly `0 records fetched`, and no parameter line;
   - no block, or a block in which **every relation is a system relation** (on the decision 5 list or with a non-zero system flag in the agent's latest catalog read; never by name prefix, security review M5 of 2026-10-24). Relations on the always-reported lists, and views over them (R12 item 5), are allowed and keep their name and mark.
2. **What it becomes**: the event of R40 item 1, with the text's signals (`signature.backup_database` for the four DDL forms of R36 item 1, `signature.remote_data_access` for `CREATE TABLE … EXTERNAL FILE`) and `rows` 0. It opens no split window, is not counted as ambiguous and does not cap the level. DDL trigger records fold into it as R26 says; the shape is judged on the record's own block, before the union with a folded group (the order of R33 item 6).
3. **Everything else keeps R20 (ambiguous)**: a DDL or DCL record without a statement line whose block names a user relation (a DDL trigger's table included), whose `records fetched` is not 0, or that has a parameter line; and a record without a statement line of any kind outside R8 case (b), R24 item 3, R34 item 1 and item 1 here.
4. **Measurement 3o** confirms the form on both versions and in SuperServer and SuperClassic, before the part of the Audit lot that applies item 1 merges: every DDL and DCL kind of the statement analysis's closed list (`GRANT`, `REVOKE`, `CREATE ROLE`, `CREATE` / `ALTER MAPPING`, `ALTER USER`, `COMMENT ON`, `RECREATE`, packages, sequences, domains, indexes, exceptions), through `isql` and through the connector's own wire client (with and without a prepared statement), whether a DML statement can be written in this form, the `records fetched` written for statements that return data without a cursor (`EXECUTE PROCEDURE` outputs, `EXECUTE BLOCK` without `SUSPEND`, `… RETURNING`, immediate-execution singletons; security review L1), and the blocks of DDL that reads user rows (`CREATE INDEX`, `ALTER INDEX … ACTIVE`, constraint and `NOT NULL` validation). A kind whose form differs from item 1 stays ambiguous (loud), never lost.

**Forging** (as in H-1). A forger can end its own record with a fake trailer and inject a header of this form with a DDL text:
- the forged DDL record gives the DDL event (`*`, always reported) under the forged binding: over-reporting, nothing beyond H-1's fake full trailer;
- a real trailer captured under the forged DDL header is well formed only with `0 records fetched` and a block of system relations only, or none. "No parameter line" is **no barrier** (security review L1): a forger that injects the header in its **last** parameter value leaves no parameter line after it. The barriers are the block and the count. A real read of a user table (its block names the table) or a real read with `records fetched` above 0 is ambiguous. What can be captured quietly is a statement that touched only system relations and wrote `0 records fetched`; it is reported as `*`, always reported, **whatever it returned**, with its always-reported relations named and marked. Statements that can return data while writing `0 records fetched` (`EXECUTE PROCEDURE` output parameters, `EXECUTE BLOCK` without `SUSPEND`, `… RETURNING`, singleton results of immediate execution) are measured in 3o; whatever 3o shows, the captured read is still reported as `*`. Only the principal can be wrong: the accepted misattribution residual;
- a header injected inside a real DDL text (a PSQL body of `CREATE PROCEDURE`) splits that record: the real header's part has no trailer, so it is ambiguous and opens a window (H1 fix 2), unchanged.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Keep R20: a 24 h cap at every `isql` schema change. (b) Also allow user relations in the block when the DDL trigger records folded into the record name them: open question 2. (c) Allow the form for every kind with `0 records fetched` and a system block: a DML statement in this form was not measured (3o).

**Residual risks.** On a database with DDL triggers that touch user tables, every DDL statement written in this form is ambiguous (loud, capping the level), until a later refinement after 3o (open question 2). So is DDL that reads user rows (`CREATE INDEX`, `ALTER INDEX … ACTIVE`, constraint and `NOT NULL` validation: its block names the user table), measured in 3o (security review L1).

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol; fewer ambiguous records.

### R38. `shape.full_table_read` on noise relations

**Implemented** (after M4 of the review of the first part of lot 3): `volume.large_result` is raised whenever `records fetched` exceeds 10 000, whatever the objects. A case (a) record whose objects are all noise then names its virtual relations (R20); any other record with no object left names `*` and is always reported (R36 item 1). `shape.full_table_read` is raised only when an object **other than a noise relation** is left after the filters (relations on the always-reported lists count as objects).

Raising `shape.full_table_read` on noise relations too would, through R36 item 1, turn every catalog read without a top-level `WHERE` into an always-reported `*` event: the catalog browsing of every administration tool (`SELECT … FROM RDB$RELATIONS`), and the agent's own catalog reads whenever they are not credited (R39 (a)).

**Proposed**, amending decision 6 "Signals": `shape.full_table_read` is a read without top-level `WHERE`, aggregation or `FIRST` / `ROWS` / `FETCH FIRST` of at most 10 000 rows **that names at least one object left after the filters**. `volume.large_result` is unchanged.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Raise the shape signal on noise relations: an always-reported `*` event for every catalog browse. (b) Raise it but exempt it from R36 item 1: a signal that gives no event contradicts R36.

**Residual risks** (for docs/08). A full read of system relations (persistent catalog relations or virtual relations) of at most 10 000 rows raises no shape signal and, with no object left, gives no event (R8 item 3 and R12 item 3, as accepted). This includes the source-text columns of open question 7 of the accepted text. Reads of always-reported relations keep their name, their mark and the signal. Until R39 (k) is fixed, this residual also covers user relations that the implementation misclassifies as system relations by their name prefix (security review M2).

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol.

### R39. Interim simplifications of the lot 3 implementation

Each simplification fails closed (over-reporting, an ambiguous record or a lower level) except where stated. For each, the proposed answer is given: **accepted** as a residual until a follow-up lot, or **required** before the Audit lot merges.

- **(a) No persisted parser checkpoint** (R25 items 7 and 9, L10 and L11 of the third pass of 2026-10-26 c, R1 rule 11's persisted state). Implemented: the persisted position is held back to the first record of a pending group or of the record in progress, and after a restart the records up to the previous read end are re-read for state only, emitting nothing. Not implemented: the checkpoint of parser state, of the inputs that do not come from the log (credits, follow-ups, `check()` results), and R1 rule 11's persisted bindings, run, remembered pids and watermarks. The own-attachment and credit ledger is in memory only. **Effect**: after an agent restart the re-read starts with no state. Bindings are learned (never own), and the records of the previous agent process's attachments not yet read are reported as any client's: its catalog reads mostly as noise (no event), its reads of always-reported relations and its sampling reads of user tables as reads by the agent's account (over-reporting). **Proposed**: accepted for the first release, with the checkpoint in a follow-up lot and the effect stated in docs/08, on **one condition required before the merge** (security review M1). Without a checkpoint, a record re-read for state only can be judged differently from the first reading (a learned binding, a group ended by a bound, an ambiguous record), so emitting nothing for it could drop an event the previous process never emitted. Before the merge:
  - a re-read record emits nothing **only** if its **end** is at or before the saved read end **and** its events were durably spooled before the restart (R25 item 7's durability order);
  - the record in progress at the restart, and every event that was held, in a pending group or otherwise not released, is **re-emitted** (duplicates, the over-reporting R25 item 9 already accepts past its cap); the core tailer's replay already keeps the bytes for this (`Replay.keep`);
  - required tests: a restart in the middle of a record on an active log; a restart during a block-less hold, with records of other bindings held; a restart with a pending group, and with an ambiguous record, inside the re-read region.
- **(b) R1 rule 2 without the stop.** Implemented: a record of a pid other than the confirmed run's, and the records after a start-time change of the same pid until the next confirmation, are ambiguous; failed pids are remembered; the stream never stops. **Effect**: each server restart makes the records between the restart and the next `check()` ambiguous (always reported with `*`), and caps the level for 24 h. No read is hidden. **Proposed**: accepted, with the stop in a follow-up lot; docs/08 states that a server restart caps the level at Limited for 24 h until then.
- **(c) R29 not implemented.** `<internal>` attachment lines with a user stay ambiguous, as R18 item 3 accepted: each use of a local external data source on a declared database caps the level. **Proposed**: accepted, R29 in a follow-up lot.
- **(d) R1 rule 9 not implemented.** Since the acceptance of 2026-10-27 (open question 9 of the refinement of 2026-10-26 c, with open question 7 of 2026-10-25), rule 9 no longer raises the cost of misattribution in general and is kept for the TCP lines it still catches. Without it, a forged TCP `ATTACH_DATABASE` that copies a live binding's address and port, or names a port with no server-side socket, is not ambiguous. The misattribution residual as accepted (quiet, needing no guess) is unchanged in kind; a forger needs no held connection for the TCP route. Since R29 is not implemented either, the `<internal>` route stays ambiguous meanwhile. **Proposed**: rule 9 in the follow-up lot with R29, with the `service_mgr` socket check of R32 item 4 (N7).
- **(e) `ERROR` records give no event, and R22 / R35 follow-ups are not checked.** Implemented: `ERROR` and `WARNING` records are parsed (GDS codes kept, texts dropped; a trailer under one is ambiguous, R22 item 7) and produce no event, on every attachment. Not implemented: the follow-ups of R22 items 5 to 8 and R35 (an own-attachment follow-up-shaped `ERROR` record with no follow-up left is not made ambiguous). **Effect**: no read is hidden. An `ERROR` record carries no statement text, block or row count (a trailer under one is ambiguous), and the read, failed or not, is reported from its finish record. What is lost is the loud mark of a forged second follow-up, which hides nothing, and an event for other clients' error records, which add only GDS codes to the failed finish record already reported. **Proposed**: accepted **on one condition before the merge** (security review L2). A DSQL read refused for lack of rights (GDS `335544352`) was not measured: if the server writes it as an `ERROR` record with no finish record, it would give no event at all. Either 3n or 3o measures that form before the merge, or, the proposed default, an `ERROR` record of a binding that is not the agent's own and that carries a permission code gives an always-reported `*` event of its database under its principal (the GDS code only, texts dropped). docs/08 states that other error records give no event of their own.
- **(f) R12 item 5's view-dependency read is not implemented.** No view is known to depend on an always-reported relation, and R28 item 3 knows no block-blind view (fail closed: such reads with rows are ambiguous). This one **hides the always-reported mark**. A view over a persistent relation and an always-reported virtual relation (`MON$STATEMENTS`, `MON$CONTEXT_VARIABLES`, `RDB$CONFIG`, the `SEC$` credential relations) writes a block naming only the persistent relation (R28). Its read is then a well-formed record reported as the view and that relation, with neither `*` nor the mark. **Proposed**: **required before the merge**, with the R20 row of its constant texts and R28 item 4's invalidation at view DDL.
- **(g) The own-statement table is approximated.** Implemented: an own record's block must lie within the relations its text names plus `RDB$DATABASE` and `RDB$RELATIONS`, for every text, with the credit's fetch bounds of R22. Not implemented: the must-read sets (R13 item 1), the per-text metadata-loading sets (R23 item 1) and the R20 table test. **Assessment** (corrected after security review L3): a read of a user table other than a live sampling credit's table cannot be credited as the agent's own. The text must be exactly a text of the closed set or the sampling text of a live credit, and constant texts name no user table. A captured real read of **the sampled table**, within one sampling credit's bounds (R22), can be credited: that is the R13 and R22 residual as accepted. The forger's real text is still reported as its own record, and the agent's real record of that execution finds no credit and is ambiguous (loud). What widens is the L2 residual of R13. With no must-read set, a block-less copy of **any** own text passes, not only of texts with an empty must-read set, so a forger who has guessed a live agent token, id and transaction can pass a read of any virtual relation (always-reported ones included), within one credit's counted rows, which for a sampling credit can be the whole sample. As before, it consumes the credit, so the agent's real record is ambiguous (loud). **Proposed**: **required before the merge**: the per-text sets of R13 and R23 and the R20 table test, since the widened residual concerns always-reported relations and the sets are accepted obligations.
- **(h) R9's exclusion applies to Audit only.** Discovery still scans a declared database that is its own security database, or that is not shown not to be one, while the note `coverage.security_database_excluded` says that such a database is "not read by Discovery or Audit". **Proposed**: **required before the merge**: Discovery must not sample a security database (R9: out of scope for Discovery and Audit alike).
- **(i) The readers check is partial** (R4, proof 1). Implemented: the log and its directory (mode bits, owner, group, access ACLs with the mask, group members; no Partial when the agent runs in a container). Not implemented: the directory's default ACL (`system.posix_acl_default`, which gives the next log file after a rotation its access), the rotated files still tracked, and the server's own uid, which defaults to 84 (the images' `firebird` uid) when `peer_uid` is not declared. On another layout, an unrelated account with uid 84 would count as the server, and a server under another uid would make the log look readable by others. **Proposed**: **required before the merge**: the server uid from the server process that proof 2 finds (`/proc/<pid>/status`) or the declared `peer_uid`, never a default, plus the default ACL and the rotated files, as R4 lists them. The check decides Partial.
- **(j) `check()` with `firebird.audit` is not exercised end to end.** The integration harness runs the agent on the host against the pinned servers; no job runs the agent in its container with `firebird.audit` against a server whose trace it reads. **Proposed**: **required before docs/08 and the user guide state a Firebird Audit level, and a release gate** (security review L5): since its acceptance, [RELEASE.md](../../RELEASE.md#open-release-gates) lists the `e2e-firebird` run (P10-D) as a gate of the next release that contains the Firebird Audit lot: Partial reached, a full read of a seeded table raising an incident, the I2 check and the #82 marker cases. The merge into `dev` may precede it.
- **(k) Relations unknown to the catalog read are classed by name prefix** (security review M2). Implemented: after a complete catalog read, a name absent from its user relations is classed as virtual when it starts with `MON$` or `SEC$` or is `RDB$CONFIG`, and as a system relation (noise) when it starts with `RDB$` or `PLG$`. This contradicts security review M5 of 2026-10-24 ("the list or the system flag decides, never the name prefix alone"): a user table created after the last catalog read with such a name is dropped as noise, or treated as virtual. **Proposed**: **required before the merge**: a relation is a system relation only when it is on the explicit per-version list or was seen with a non-zero system flag, and virtual only when it is on R8's per-version virtual list; any other name unknown to the last catalog read is an object (unknown class, reported). Optionally, after a DDL record, names missing from the catalog are unknown until the next read.

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol. docs/08 states the effects of (a), (b), (c) and (e) for as long as they last, and that attachments made while the run is unconfirmed are learned after the confirmation, within the misattribution residual (security review N2).

### R40. Implementation decisions recorded, and one amendment of decision 6

1. **DDL and DCL events.** A well-formed DDL or DCL statement record gives an event of its kind (decision 6: kind only, never text) under its principal, naming `*` of its database with the always-reported mark, with the block's relations under the filters (system relations outside the always-reported lists are noise, R24 item 2) and the text's signals; password-bearing texts the same, kind only (the #82 rule). This follows the DDL / DCL handling of [ADR-0051](0051-sqlserver-connector.md) and only over-reports.
2. **The clock offset** (amending decision 6 "What is kept", which learns it from the read-back probe). The implementation learns it from the agent's own `ATTACH_DATABASE` records (the connector's attach time minus the record's printed time, in whole seconds), re-learned at every own attachment; the probe stays proof 7. Before the first own attachment of a run is read, times are taken as printed. A forged copy of an own `ATTACH_DATABASE` could set the offset, which moves every event's time and the time-based own and credit rules. **Required before the merge** (security review M3):
   - the offset is learned only from an own `ATTACH_DATABASE` that is not inside a split window and not ambiguous;
   - it is **provisional** until a credited own record on that binding (or the proof 7 probe) confirms it, and **reverted** if the binding is poisoned;
   - an offset more than a few seconds away from the host's own UTC offset is rejected (the previous one is kept).
3. **The own-attachment and credit ledger** is one per server endpoint (socket address), shared by `check()` and the scans, keyed by the declared `attach` and the attachment id, bounded (65 536 credits, 4 096 attachments, a credit life of 15 minutes as the outer bound of decision 6), in memory (R39 (a)).
4. **Before the first `check()`**, the first pid seen is taken provisionally as the run; the first `check()` confirms it or ends it, and records of another pid are ambiguous meanwhile (R39 (b)). While the provisional start time is unknown, the first `check()` compares **only the pid** (security review N1), so a confirmed provisional run is not ended at the first check. A forged provisional pid is loud only: the real pid's records are ambiguous until that check.
5. **Ambiguous records of the form without a statement line** keep the text's `signature.backup_database` and `signature.remote_data_access` (R36 item 1: a signal only adds reporting).
6. **Configuration** (R2, decision 6 "Configuration"):
   - the key `firebird.audit.root` names the Firebird root (`$(root)`);
   - `firebird_conf` must be `<root>/firebird.conf` and `databases.conf` must be `<root>/databases.conf`, the files the server reads; the log, the trace configuration and `firebird.conf` must be distinct files;
   - in the trace configuration, booleans are accepted unquoted only and trace keys in their exact case; `firebird.conf` and `databases.conf` keys and macros are compared without case, as the server does. An engine-matrix test checks each boolean spelling the agent accepts or refuses against what the server applies (security review N5);
   - a path with a `..` component or a backslash is refused (never matched);
   - the macros `$(root)`, `$(install)`, `$(this)`, `$(dir_conf)` and `$(dir_secDb)` are resolved to the root, as in the official images and the tarball install, where they coincide; any other macro is not resolved (not evaluated, R2 rule 2). Distribution packages that place these directories apart are documented in the user guide as layouts for which the agent's resolution does not hold. **A fail-closed layout check, required before the merge** (security review L4): `check()` compares the declared root with the directory of the server binary in the server process's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` (readable without privileges; the process proof 2 finds); a binary outside `<root>/bin`, or an unreadable command line, makes proof 2 not proven (Limited).

### Obligations still open after this refinement

- **3o** (R37), on 4.0.7 and 5.0.4, SuperServer and SuperClassic, before the part of the Audit lot that applies R37 item 1 merges.
- **Before the Audit lot merges**: R39 (a)'s re-read condition and its tests, (e)'s condition (the refused-read measurement or the permission-code event), (f), (g), (h), (i) and (k); R40 item 2's offset conditions and item 6's layout check.
- **Before docs/08 and the user guide state a Firebird Audit level, and as a release gate**: (j), the `e2e-firebird` job (P10-D), listed in RELEASE.md.
- **Follow-up lot** (R39): the parser checkpoint, R1 rule 2's stop, R29 with R1 rule 9, the R22 / R35 follow-ups.
- Unchanged: **3n** (R32 to R34), **3l** and **3m** with **8g**, and obligation 15's open items.

### Protocol (P10-C)

**No protocol change.** R37 and R38 are parser rules of the agent; R39 (h) uses the existing note `coverage.security_database_excluded`, and R39 (e)'s permission-code event is an ordinary `AccessEvent` naming `*`. No note code, label, field, signal, capability token or metric is added. The Audit lot keeps its `security-reviewer` review (AGENTS.md).

### Security review of 2026-10-30 (refinement of 2026-10-30)

Review of the first version of this refinement (`a7b7666`); verdict: **sound after changes**, no Critical or High finding in the text. Every finding is applied in this revision:

| Finding | Fix |
|---|---|
| **M1**: R39 (a)'s merge condition could still drop the record in progress and held events | R39 (a): a re-read record emits nothing only if its end is at or before the saved read end and its events were durably spooled; the record in progress and every held, pending or unreleased event re-emitted (`Replay.keep`); tests for a restart mid-record, during a block-less hold, with a pending group and with an ambiguous record |
| **M2**: unknown relations classed by name prefix, against M5 of 2026-10-24 | R39 (k), required before the merge: system or virtual only from the per-version lists or a seen system flag, any other unknown name an object; R38's residual covers misclassified relations until then |
| **M3**: the clock offset can be set by a forged own attach | R40 item 2, required before the merge: learned only outside windows and from records that are not ambiguous, provisional until a credited own record or the probe confirms it, reverted on poisoning, bounded by the host's UTC offset; the heading states that it amends decision 6 |
| **L1** | R37: "no parameter line" is no barrier (injection in the last parameter value); captured reads reported as `*` whatever they returned; 3o gains statements returning data without a cursor and DDL that reads user rows, the latter a residual |
| **L2** | R39 (e): before the merge, the refused-read form (`335544352`) measured, or (default) a permission-code `ERROR` record of a non-own binding gives an always-reported `*` event |
| **L3** | R39 (g): a captured read of the sampled table within one sampling credit can be credited (the R13 / R22 residual), the agent's real record then ambiguous |
| **L4** | R40 item 6: the declared root compared with the server binary's directory from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, mismatch: proof 2 not proven; open question 6 answered |
| **L5** | R39 (j): the `e2e-firebird` run a RELEASE.md release gate once accepted |
| **N1** | R40 item 4: only the pid compared while the provisional start is unknown; a forged provisional pid loud only |
| **N2** | R39 "Effect": docs/08 states that attachments made while the run is unconfirmed are learned after the confirmation |
| **N5** | R40 item 6: an engine-matrix test per boolean spelling |

### Open questions (for the maintainer, refinement of 2026-10-30)

Each with the answer this refinement proposed, revised to the security review of 2026-10-30. **Decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-30: every proposed answer accepted.**

1. **R37**: a DDL or DCL record without a `Statement <n>:` line is well formed with exactly `0 records fetched` and a block of system relations only (or none), after 3o; everything else in that form stays ambiguous?
   **Proposed**: yes. A captured real read is ambiguous or reported as `*`, always reported, whatever it returned; otherwise each `isql` schema change caps the level for 24 h.
2. **R37, alternative (b)**: also accept user relations in such a block when the DDL trigger records folded into it name them?
   **Proposed**: not now; decide after 3o, in a later refinement with its own review.
3. **R38**: `shape.full_table_read` only when an object other than a noise relation is left, with the residual stated in docs/08?
   **Proposed**: yes, with R39 (k) fixed before the merge.
4. **R39 (a) to (e)**: accept the missing checkpoint, the run change without a stop, R29 and R1 rule 9 left to a follow-up lot, and `ERROR` records with no event, with their effects in docs/08, on the conditions of (a) (re-read emits nothing only for spooled records ending before the saved end; held and in-progress events re-emitted; the four tests) and (e) (a permission-code `ERROR` record of a non-own binding gives an always-reported `*` event, unless 3n / 3o shows the refused read has a finish record)?
   **Proposed**: yes.
5. **R39 (f) to (k) and R40 items 2 and 6**: the view-dependency read, the per-text own-statement sets, Discovery's R9 exclusion, the full readers check, no prefix classing, the guarded clock offset and the layout check required before the merge; the `e2e-firebird` run required before the docs state a Firebird Audit level and listed as a RELEASE.md release gate?
   **Proposed**: yes. Each either hides a mark or a relation, contradicts an accepted rule, or decides Partial.
6. **R40 item 6**: is the `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` layout check enough, with distribution layouts documented as not evaluated (Limited)?
   **Proposed**: yes; a configurable layout can come in a later refinement if operators ask.

## Refinement (2026-10-30 b, proposed, awaiting the maintainer's decision): the lot 3 review

Status: **Proposed on 2026-10-30, awaiting the maintainer's decision.** It answers two findings of the full security review of the lot 3 branch (the Audit lot, `feat/p10-b-firebird-audit`, draft pull request #385): M6 (reads of always-reported relations behind code or a computed column keep no mark) and L1 (the conditions under which the implementation accepts a learned clock offset differ from the accepted text). It amends, by reference:

- the refinement of 2026-10-26 b: R12 item 5 (views over always-reported relations), with R28 item 4 (DDL invalidates the sets) and R39 (f);
- the refinement of 2026-10-30: R40 item 2 (the clock offset), its third condition.

The accepted text and the six accepted refinements stay as they are; where this refinement differs from them, this refinement applies. **No protocol change.** ROADMAP: [phase 10](../ROADMAP.md#phase-10--engine-scope-wave-2-sqlite-firebird), P10-B.

### R41. Always-reported relations behind routines and computed columns (review M6)

**Observed** (review of the lot 3 branch, against the accepted text): R12 item 5 marks reads of **views** that depend on an always-reported relation. Two other paths read an always-reported relation with no mark:

- a **procedure** that reads one: `SELECT * FROM P`, where `P` selects `MON$SQL_TEXT` from `MON$STATEMENTS`. Reads of virtual relations write no block (measurement 3b, R28), so the record names `P` (and the persistent relations the procedure touched);
- a **computed column**: `CREATE TABLE T (C COMPUTED BY ((SELECT LIST(MON$SQL_TEXT) FROM MON$STATEMENTS)))`, recorded in `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` with dependent type 3. A read of `T` names `T` only.

In both cases the read is reported as `P` or `T`, with no `*` and no always-reported mark, so a policy on the statement-text or credential-bearing lists does not see it. Functions called inside such code and packaged routines give the same path. A function called directly in a statement text is already covered: a call of a function that is not built in adds `*` and is always reported (decision 6, "Code that runs out of sight"). So is dynamic SQL: an `EXECUTE STATEMENT` text inside a routine, and a UDR's internal queries, are written as statement records of their own (7c, R18), and their texts name the relation.

**Proposed**, amending R12 item 5 (and R28 item 4):

1. **The dependent set.** At every re-evaluation, `check()` computes from the public catalog the set of objects of the database that depend, directly or transitively, on a relation of the statement-text or credential-bearing always-reported lists:
   - views (R12 item 5, unchanged);
   - procedures (dependent type 5), functions (15), packages (18 and 19; a package is dependent when its header or body is), triggers (2), and **tables with a computed column** (3, mapped to the table through `RDB$RELATION_FIELDS.RDB$FIELD_SOURCE`). A table is dependent when any of its computed columns is.
   
   The edges are the rows of `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` and `RDB$VIEW_RELATIONS`. Routines can form cycles (recursive procedures, packages that call each other). The set is computed over the strongly connected components of the graph: every member of a component is dependent when any member is, or when a component it depends on is. A reverse reachability from the always-reported relations with a visited set gives the same result.
2. **Bounds, fail closed.** The read and the computation are bounded: at most 65 536 dependency rows and 16 384 dependent objects per database, R12 item 5's view bounds unchanged, with the read under the statement timeout. A cut or failed read, or a computed-column row that maps to no table, makes **every** view, procedure, function, package and trigger of the database, and every table with a computed column, count as dependent until the next complete read (over-reporting, loud). The agent logs the cut. The reads are new constant catalog texts of `sql::STATEMENTS`, with their R20 rows and their R13 and R23 sets (R39 (g)).
3. **What a record naming a dependent object becomes.** A record that names an object of the dependent set gets `*` and the always-reported mark, in every shape (R12's no-record shape, a block-ful record, a folded routine record, an event of its own under R4). It is never the agent's own, with one exception: Discovery's exact sampling text of a table with a dependent computed column stays creditable only when it names none of the dependent computed columns. Discovery leaves those columns out of its sampling texts: it already lists columns from `RDB$RELATION_FIELDS`, and sampling them would be a read of the always-reported relation by the agent.
4. **How a name is matched.** A record can name the object in three places: in its **text** (both dialect readings, R8 item 1: an unquoted identifier upper-cased, a quoted identifier as written); in its **block** (the relation names as the server prints them); and in the **name line of a routine record** folded into it or reported on its own (`Procedure <name>:`, `Function <name>:`, `Trigger <name> FOR <relation> (<kind>):` and the 4.0.7 forms, parsed from the right end as R14 item 2 says). A selectable procedure read appears in the text and, when the procedure ran, as a routine record of the same binding and transaction. For a packaged routine, the package part (before the first dot outside quotes) is matched against the dependent packages. Names are compared:
   - on the names as read from the log, before the ADR-0009 normalization;
   - exactly (case-sensitive, no prefix match), against the catalog names with their trailing blanks removed;
   - without regard to kind: a name equal to any dependent object of any kind matches (over-reporting only).
   
   A name line that does not parse is already ambiguous (R14 item 2).
5. **Any DDL invalidates the set** (review L5, widening R28 item 4 from view DDL to every DDL). Any DDL statement record on a declared database invalidates that database's dependent set and R28's block-blind set, from that record's position until the next `check()`. This includes the form without a statement line (R37), a DDL text that does not parse and an ambiguous DDL record. In between:
   - no view is block-blind;
   - every view, procedure, function, package and trigger counts as dependent;
   - so does every table with a computed column at the last read, every table named by a DDL text in that interval, and every table unknown to the last catalog read;
   - a DDL text that does not parse, or an ambiguous DDL record, makes every relation of the database count as dependent.
   
   Such a record asks for an early `check()`, merged under R11 8d's limit (at most one per 30 s per target, never while a check runs).
6. **Required before the lot 3 merge**, with R39 (f). Tests: the two cases of the review (`SELECT * FROM P` over `MON$STATEMENTS`; the computed column of dependent type 3), a call cycle, a packaged procedure, a trigger that reads `MON$CONTEXT_VARIABLES`, a cut dependency read, and a `CREATE PROCEDURE` followed by its call before the next `check()`. Run on 4.0.7 and 5.0.4, they confirm the dependent types and the computed-column mapping.

**Forging.** A forged record that names a dependent object only adds a mark (over-reporting). A forged DDL record only invalidates the sets: more marked events until the next `check()`, at most one extra check per 30 s. This is R28's forging note, unchanged in kind.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) Mark per computed column instead of per table: the text does not show the columns of `SELECT *`, and the block names tables only. (b) Read routine bodies (BLR or source) to find reads: decision 6 keeps the Audit path off routine definitions, and `RDB$DEPENDENCIES` already holds what the server recorded at compile time. (c) Leave routines out and state a residual: the read is quiet and concerns the always-reported lists.

**Residual risks.**
- A routine or table changed through a path that writes no DDL record in the declared log is caught at the next `check()` (as R28).
- Data that code copies from an always-reported relation into a persistent table is reported as a write of that table by the code's caller. A later read of that table is an ordinary read: it names that table with no mark.
- A database whose ordinary tables carry such computed columns, or whose dependency read is cut, gets every read of those tables always reported (over-reporting, loud).

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol; more always-reported events.

### R42. The clock offset's acceptance window (review L1)

**Observed** (lot 3 implementation): R40 item 2 rejects a learned offset "more than a few seconds away from the host's own UTC offset". In the official images the server often runs in a container with `TZ` unset (UTC) while the host has another zone, so the host's UTC offset says nothing about the time zone the server prints in. The implementation instead accepts a measured offset only when it lies **within 5 s of a whole quarter hour**, with an absolute value of **at most 14 h**. Every UTC offset in use is a multiple of 15 minutes, between −12 h and +14 h. Connections are loopback only (decision 3), so the server and the agent read the same host clock, and the 5 s cover the time between the connector's attach and the printed time.

The review finds this acceptable in substance:
- a forged copy of an own `ATTACH_DATABASE` collides with the real record of that attachment: a duplicate, so the binding is poisoned and the offset reverted (R40 item 2);
- an offset is only confirmed by a credited own record on that binding, or by the proof 7 read-back probe.

**Proposed**, amending R40 item 2, its third condition:

1. A measured offset is rejected (the previous one is kept) unless it lies within 5 s of a whole multiple of 15 minutes, with an absolute value of at most 14 h. The test bounds which measured offsets are accepted; it does not round them. The first two conditions of R40 item 2 (no window, not ambiguous; provisional until confirmed, reverted on poisoning) are unchanged.
2. **A confirmed offset changes by at most 1 h at a time** (a daylight-saving change). A measured offset further from the confirmed one stays pending and is not applied until the proof 7 read-back probe re-confirms it. Meanwhile the confirmed offset is kept and an early `check()` is asked for, merged under R11 8d's limit. A change within 1 h is provisional until confirmed, as any learned offset.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) The host's UTC offset, as accepted: ill-defined when the server runs in another zone than the host, which is the common container layout. (b) The probe alone: an offset only at `check()` time, and none before the first one, leaves times as printed for longer. (c) A declared server time zone: one more key to keep in step with the server's configuration.

**Residual risks.** Until a forged offset is reverted, it can move event times only to a quarter-hour grid within 14 h, and by at most 1 h from a confirmed offset; the reported reads and their objects do not change. A server clock more than 5 s off the grid gets no offset: times are taken as printed, and the time-based own and credit rules fail, so own records are not credited (over-reporting, loud).

**Effect.** No change to levels, notes or protocol.

### Obligations still open after this refinement

- **Before the Audit lot merges**, added to the list of the refinement of 2026-10-30: R41 with its tests (with R39 (f)), and R42's window and change limit (with R40 item 2).
- Unchanged: the other items of [the list of the refinement of 2026-10-30](#obligations-still-open-after-this-refinement-3).

### Protocol (P10-C)

**No protocol change.** R41 adds constant catalog texts and parser rules of the agent; a marked event is an ordinary `AccessEvent` naming `*` with the always-reported mark. R42 is an agent rule. No note code, label, field, signal, capability token or metric is added. The Audit lot keeps its `security-reviewer` review (AGENTS.md).

### Open questions (for the maintainer, refinement of 2026-10-30 b)

1. **R41**: extend R12 item 5's dependent set to procedures, functions, packages, triggers and tables with computed columns, computed transitively over strongly connected components, and mark every record that names one with `*` and the always-reported mark?
   **Proposed**: yes, required before the lot 3 merge.
2. **R41, granularity**: mark every read of a table with a dependent computed column (table level), rather than per column?
   **Proposed**: yes; the text and the block do not show columns reliably. Discovery leaves those columns out of its sampling texts.
3. **R41, cut read**: count every view, routine, package, trigger and table with a computed column as dependent until the next complete read, with a log line and no new note?
   **Proposed**: yes; a note code can come in a later refinement if cuts are seen in practice.
4. **R41, DDL**: invalidate the sets at any DDL record (not only view DDL), with the scope of item 5 and an early `check()`?
   **Proposed**: yes (review L5).
5. **R42**: accept a measured offset only within 5 s of a whole quarter hour and at most 14 h, and limit a change of a confirmed offset to 1 h unless the proof 7 probe re-confirms it?
   **Proposed**: yes.
